### Q1. JEE Main 2026 (08 April Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Solid carbon, CaO and CaCO$_3$ are mixed and allowed to attain equilibrium at T K.
$\text{CaCO}_3(s) \rightleftharpoons \text{CaO}(s) + \text{CO}_2(g) \quad K_{p_1} = 0.08$ atm
$\text{C}(s) + \text{CO}_2(g) \rightleftharpoons 2\text{CO}(g) \quad K_{p_2} = 2$ atm
The partial pressure of CO is ________ $\times 10^{-1}$ atm

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **4**

**Detailed Solution:**

For the first equilibrium:

$\text{CaCO}_3(s) \rightleftharpoons \text{CaO}(s) + \text{CO}_2(g)$

$K_{p_1} = P_{\text{CO}_2} = 0.08 \text{ atm}$

For the second equilibrium:

$\text{C}(s) + \text{CO}_2(g) \rightleftharpoons 2\text{CO}(g)$

$K_{p_2} = \dfrac{(P_{\text{CO}})^2}{P_{\text{CO}_2}} = 2 \text{ atm}$

Substituting the value of $P_{\text{CO}_2}$:

$\dfrac{(P_{\text{CO}})^2}{0.08} = 2$

$(P_{\text{CO}})^2 = 0.16$

$P_{\text{CO}} = 0.4 \text{ atm} = 4 \times 10^{-1} \text{ atm}$

Answer: $4$

</details>

### Q2. JEE Main 2026 (08 April Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Consider the following reactions in which all the reactants and products are present in gaseous state
$2xy \rightleftharpoons x_2 + y_2 \quad K_1 = 2.5\times 10^5$
$xy + \dfrac{1}{2}z_2 \rightleftharpoons xyz \quad K_2 = 5\times 10^{-3}$
The value of $K_3$ for the equilibrium $\dfrac{1}{2}x_2 + \dfrac{1}{2}y_2 + \dfrac{1}{2}z_2 \rightleftharpoons xyz$ is:

**Options:**

- **(A)** $2.5\times 10^{-3}$
- **(B)** $2.5\times 10^{3}$
- **(C)** $1.0\times 10^{-5}$
- **(D)** $5\times 10^{-3}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $1.0\times 10^{-5}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The given reactions are:
$2xy \rightleftharpoons x_2 + y_2 \quad K_1 = 2.5 \times 10^5$
$xy + \dfrac{1}{2}z_2 \rightleftharpoons xyz \quad K_2 = 5 \times 10^{-3}$

We need to find the equilibrium constant $K_3$ for the reaction:
$\dfrac{1}{2}x_2 + \dfrac{1}{2}y_2 + \dfrac{1}{2}z_2 \rightleftharpoons xyz$

Reversing the first reaction and multiplying it by $\dfrac{1}{2}$, we get:
$\dfrac{1}{2}x_2 + \dfrac{1}{2}y_2 \rightleftharpoons xy$
The equilibrium constant for this modified reaction is:
$K' = \left(\dfrac{1}{K_1}\right)^{\dfrac{1}{2}} = \dfrac{1}{\sqrt{K_1}}$

Adding this modified reaction to the second reaction:
$\dfrac{1}{2}x_2 + \dfrac{1}{2}y_2 \rightleftharpoons xy \quad (K')$
$xy + \dfrac{1}{2}z_2 \rightleftharpoons xyz \quad (K_2)$
---------------------------------------------------
$\dfrac{1}{2}x_2 + \dfrac{1}{2}y_2 + \dfrac{1}{2}z_2 \rightleftharpoons xyz$

The equilibrium constant $K_3$ for the overall reaction is the product of the equilibrium constants of the added reactions:
$K_3 = K' \times K_2 = \dfrac{K_2}{\sqrt{K_1}}$

Substituting the given values:
$K_3 = \dfrac{5 \times 10^{-3}}{\sqrt{2.5 \times 10^5}}$

$K_3 = \dfrac{5 \times 10^{-3}}{\sqrt{25 \times 10^4}}$

$K_3 = \dfrac{5 \times 10^{-3}}{5 \times 10^2}$

$K_3 = 1.0 \times 10^{-5}$

Answer: $1.0\times 10^{-5}$

</details>

### Q3. JEE Main 2026 (06 April Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

In a closed flask at $600$ K, one mole of X$_2$Y$_4$(g) attains equilibrium as given below :
$\text{X}_2\text{Y}_4(g) \rightleftharpoons 2\text{XY}_2(g)$
At equilibrium, $75\%$ X$_2$Y$_4$(g) was dissociated and the total pressure is $1$ atm. The magnitude of $\Delta_r G^{\ominus}$ (in kJ mol$^{-1}$) at this temperature is __________. (Nearest Integer) (Given : R $= 8.3$ J mol$^{-1}$ K$^{-1}$; $\ln 10 = 2.3$, $\log 2 = 0.3$, $\log 3 = 0.48$, $\log 5 = 0.69$, $\log 7 = 0.84$)

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **8**

**Detailed Solution:**

The given equilibrium reaction is:
$\text{X}_2\text{Y}_4(g) \rightleftharpoons 2\text{XY}_2(g)$

Initial moles: $1$
Degree of dissociation, $\alpha = 0.75$
Moles of $\text{X}_2\text{Y}_4$ at equilibrium $= 1 - \alpha = 1 - 0.75 = 0.25$
Moles of $\text{XY}_2$ at equilibrium $= 2\alpha = 2 \times 0.75 = 1.5$
Total moles at equilibrium $= 0.25 + 1.5 = 1.75$

Given total pressure, $P = 1$ atm.
Partial pressure of $\text{X}_2\text{Y}_4$, $P_{\text{X}_2\text{Y}_4} = \dfrac{0.25}{1.75} \times 1 = \dfrac{1}{7}$ atm
Partial pressure of $\text{XY}_2$, $P_{\text{XY}_2} = \dfrac{1.5}{1.75} \times 1 = \dfrac{6}{7}$ atm

The equilibrium constant $K_p$ is given by:
$K_p = \dfrac{(P_{\text{XY}_2})^2}{P_{\text{X}_2\text{Y}_4}} = \dfrac{(\dfrac{6}{7})^2}{\dfrac{1}{7}} = \dfrac{36}{7}$

The standard Gibbs free energy change is:
$\Delta_r G^{\ominus} = -RT \ln K_p = -2.3 RT \log K_p$

Calculating $\log K_p$:
$\log \left(\dfrac{36}{7}\right) = \log 36 - \log 7 = \log (2^2 \times 3^2) - \log 7$
$\log \left(\dfrac{36}{7}\right) = 2 \log 2 + 2 \log 3 - \log 7$
$\log \left(\dfrac{36}{7}\right) = 2(0.3) + 2(0.48) - 0.84 = 0.6 + 0.96 - 0.84 = 0.72$

Substituting the values into the $\Delta_r G^{\ominus}$ equation:
$\Delta_r G^{\ominus} = -2.3 \times 8.3 \times 600 \times 0.72$
$\Delta_r G^{\ominus} = -8246.88 \text{ J mol}^{-1} = -8.24688 \text{ kJ mol}^{-1}$

The magnitude of $\Delta_r G^{\ominus}$ is $8.24688 \text{ kJ mol}^{-1}$.
Rounding to the nearest integer, we get $8$.

Answer: $8$

</details>

---

### Q4. JEE Main 2026 (06 April Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

One mole each of He and $A(g)$ are taken in a $10$ L closed flask and heated to $400$ K to establish the following equilibrium. $A(g) \rightleftharpoons B(g)$. $K_c$ for this reaction at $400$ K is $4.0$. The partial pressures (in atm) of He and $B(g)$ are respectively (at equilibrium) (Assume He, $A(g)$ and $B(g)$ behave as ideal gases) (Given: $R = 0.082$ L atm K$^{-1}$ mol$^{-1}$)

**Options:**

- **(A)** $3.28, 2.624$
- **(B)** $2.624, 3.28$
- **(C)** $3.28, 0.656$
- **(D)** $0.656, 6.56$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $3.28, 2.624$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The given reaction is $A(g) \rightleftharpoons B(g)$.

Let the initial moles of $A$ be $1$ and at equilibrium, let $x$ moles of $A$ dissociate.

Moles at equilibrium:
$n_A = 1 - x$
$n_B = x$

Since $\Delta n_g = 0$, the equilibrium constant $K_c$ can be written in terms of moles:
$K_c = \dfrac{n_B}{n_A} = \dfrac{x}{1 - x} = 4.0$

$x = 4 - 4x \Rightarrow 5x = 4 \Rightarrow x = 0.8$

At equilibrium, the moles of the gases are:
$n_{He} = 1$
$n_B = 0.8$

Using the ideal gas equation $P = \dfrac{nRT}{V}$, the partial pressures are calculated as follows:

Partial pressure of He:
$P_{He} = \dfrac{1 \times 0.082 \times 400}{10} = 3.28$ atm

Partial pressure of $B(g)$:
$P_B = \dfrac{0.8 \times 0.082 \times 400}{10} = 2.624$ atm

Answer: $3.28, 2.624$

</details>

### Q5. JEE Main 2026 (05 April Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

The reaction $A(g) \rightleftharpoons B(g) + C(g)$ was initiated with the amount '$a$' of $A(g)$. At equilibrium it is found that the amount of $A(g)$ remaining is $(a - x)$ at a total pressure of $p$.
The equilibrium constant $K_p$ of the reaction can be calculated from the expression :

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\dfrac{x^2}{a^2 + x^2} \times p$
- **(B)** $\dfrac{x^2}{a^2 - x^2} \times p$
- **(C)** $\dfrac{a + x^2}{x^2} \times p$
- **(D)** $\dfrac{a^2 - x^2}{x^2} \times p$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $\dfrac{x^2}{a^2 - x^2} \times p$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The reaction is $A(g) \rightleftharpoons B(g) + C(g)$

Initial moles: $a$ for $A$, $0$ for $B$, $0$ for $C$

Moles at equilibrium: $(a - x)$ for $A$, $x$ for $B$, $x$ for $C$

Total moles at equilibrium $= (a - x) + x + x = a + x$

Partial pressures at equilibrium:

$p_A = \dfrac{a - x}{a + x} p$

$p_B = \dfrac{x}{a + x} p$

$p_C = \dfrac{x}{a + x} p$

The equilibrium constant $K_p$ is given by:

$K_p = \dfrac{p_B \cdot p_C}{p_A}$

$K_p = \dfrac{\left(\dfrac{x}{a + x} p\right) \left(\dfrac{x}{a + x} p\right)}{\dfrac{a - x}{a + x} p}$

$K_p = \dfrac{x^2}{(a + x)(a - x)} p$

$K_p = \dfrac{x^2}{a^2 - x^2} p$

Answer: $\dfrac{x^2}{a^2 - x^2} \times p$

</details>

---

### Q6. JEE Main 2026 (05 April Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

The values of pressure equilibrium constant recorded at different temperatures for the following equilibrium reaction have been given below
$A(g) \rightleftharpoons B(g) + C(g)$
<table style="border: 1px solid black; border-collapse: collapse; width: auto;"><tr><td style="border: 1px solid black; padding: 5px;">$\dfrac{1}{T}(\text{K}^{-1})$</td><td style="border: 1px solid black; padding: 5px;">$\log_{10} K_p$</td></tr><tr><td style="border: 1px solid black; padding: 5px;">$0.05$</td><td style="border: 1px solid black; padding: 5px;">$3.5$</td></tr><tr><td style="border: 1px solid black; padding: 5px;">$0.06$</td><td style="border: 1px solid black; padding: 5px;">$2.5$</td></tr><tr><td style="border: 1px solid black; padding: 5px;">$0.07$</td><td style="border: 1px solid black; padding: 5px;">$1.5$</td></tr></table>
The magnitude of $\dfrac{\Delta H°}{R}$ calculated from the above data is _____. (Nearest integer)

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **230**

**Detailed Solution:**

According to the van 't Hoff equation:

$\log_{10} K_p = -\dfrac{\Delta H^\circ}{2.303 R} \left(\dfrac{1}{T}\right) + \dfrac{\Delta S^\circ}{2.303 R}$

This represents a linear equation $y = mx + c$, where $y = \log_{10} K_p$ and $x = \dfrac{1}{T}$. The slope $m$ of the line is given by:

$m = -\dfrac{\Delta H^\circ}{2.303 R}$

From the given data table, we can calculate the slope $m$ using any two points, for example $(0.05, 3.5)$ and $(0.06, 2.5)$:

$m = \dfrac{y_2 - y_1}{x_2 - x_1} = \dfrac{2.5 - 3.5}{0.06 - 0.05} = \dfrac{-1.0}{0.01} = -100$

Equating the calculated slope to the theoretical slope:

$-\dfrac{\Delta H^\circ}{2.303 R} = -100$

$\dfrac{\Delta H^\circ}{R} = 100 \times 2.303 = 230.3$

The magnitude of $\dfrac{\Delta H^\circ}{R}$ is $230.3$. Rounding to the nearest integer, we get $230$.

Answer: $230$

</details>

### Q7. JEE Main 2026 (04 April Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

For the following reaction at $50°$C and at $2$ atm pressure,
$2N_2O_5(g) \rightleftharpoons 2N_2O_4(g)+O_2(g)$
$N_2O_5$ is $50\%$ dissociated.
The magnitude of standard free energy change at this temperature is $x$.
$x=$ ______ J mol$^{-1}$ [Nearest integer].
Given: $R=8.314$ J mol$^{-1}$ K$^{-1}$, $\log 2=0.30$, $\log 3=0.48$, $\ln 10=2.303$, $°C+273=K$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **2474**

**Detailed Solution:**

Let the initial moles of $N_2O_5$ be $2$.

The given reaction is:
$2N_2O_5(g) \rightleftharpoons 2N_2O_4(g) + O_2(g)$

Since $N_2O_5$ is $50\%$ dissociated (degree of dissociation $\alpha = 0.5$), the moles at equilibrium are:
Moles of $N_2O_5 = 2(1 - 0.5) = 1$
Moles of $N_2O_4 = 2(0.5) = 1$
Moles of $O_2 = 0.5$

Total moles at equilibrium $= 1 + 1 + 0.5 = 2.5$

Given the total pressure $P = 2$ atm, the partial pressures of the gases are:
$P_{N_2O_5} = \dfrac{1}{2.5} \times 2 = 0.8$ atm
$P_{N_2O_4} = \dfrac{1}{2.5} \times 2 = 0.8$ atm
$P_{O_2} = \dfrac{0.5}{2.5} \times 2 = 0.4$ atm

The equilibrium constant $K_p$ is:
$K_p = \dfrac{(P_{N_2O_4})^2 \cdot P_{O_2}}{(P_{N_2O_5})^2} = \dfrac{(0.8)^2 \times 0.4}{(0.8)^2} = 0.4$

The standard free energy change $\Delta G^\circ$ is given by:
$\Delta G^\circ = -RT \ln K_p$

Using the given values:
$\ln(0.4) = 2.303 \log(0.4) = 2.303 (\log 4 - \log 10)$
$\ln(0.4) = 2.303 (2 \log 2 - 1) = 2.303 (2 \times 0.30 - 1) = 2.303 (-0.4) = -0.9212$

Now, substituting the values into the $\Delta G^\circ$ equation ($T = 50^\circ C + 273 = 323$ K):
$\Delta G^\circ = -8.314 \times 323 \times (-0.9212)$
$\Delta G^\circ = 2473.81$ J mol$^{-1}$

The magnitude of the standard free energy change to the nearest integer is $2474$ J mol$^{-1}$.

Answer: $2474$

</details>

---

### Q8. JEE Main 2026 (04 April Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

At $T(K)$, the equilibrium constant of $A_2(g) + B_2(g) \rightleftharpoons C(g)$ is $2.7 \times 10^{-5}$. What is the equilibrium constant for $\dfrac{1}{3}A_2(g) + \dfrac{1}{3}B_2(g) \rightleftharpoons \dfrac{1}{3}C(g)$ at the same temperature?

**Options:**

- **(A)** $(2.7 \times 10^{-5})^3$
- **(B)** $6 \times 10^{-2}$
- **(C)** $\sqrt{2.7 \times 10^{-5}}$
- **(D)** $3 \times 10^{-2}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $3 \times 10^{-2}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

For the reaction $A_2(g) + B_2(g) \rightleftharpoons C(g)$, the equilibrium constant is $K_1 = 2.7 \times 10^{-5}$.

When a reaction is multiplied by a factor $n$, the new equilibrium constant becomes $K^n$.

The given reaction is multiplied by $\dfrac{1}{3}$ to obtain the reaction $\dfrac{1}{3}A_2(g) + \dfrac{1}{3}B_2(g) \rightleftharpoons \dfrac{1}{3}C(g)$.

The new equilibrium constant $K_2$ is given by:

$K_2 = (K_1)^{1/3}$

$K_2 = (2.7 \times 10^{-5})^{1/3}$

$K_2 = (27 \times 10^{-6})^{1/3}$

$K_2 = 3 \times 10^{-2}$

Answer: $3 \times 10^{-2}$

</details>

---

### Q9. JEE Main 2026 (28 January Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Observe the following equilibrium in a 1 L flask. 
 $\mathrm{A}(\mathrm{~g}) \rightleftharpoons \mathrm{B}(\mathrm{~g})$ 
 At $\mathrm{T}(\mathrm{K})$, the equilibrium concentrations of A and B are 0.5 M and 0.375 M respectively. 0.1 moles of A is added into the flask and heated to $\mathrm{T}(\mathrm{K})$ to establish the equilibrium again. The new equilibrium concentrations (in M) of A and B are respectively

**Options:**

- **(A)** $0.53,0.4$.
- **(B)** $0.742,0.557$.
- **(C)** $0.557,0.418$.
- **(D)** $0.367,0.275$.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $0.557,0.418$.**

**Detailed Solution:**

Initial equilibrium:
[A]=0.5 M, [B]=0.375 M; $K_c=\frac{0.375}{0.5}=0.75$
After adding 0.1 mol A: [A]=0.6 M, [B]=0.375 M; Q=0.625
Q < Kc, so equilibrium shifts right.
Let x be A that decomposes:
$\frac{0.375+x}{0.6-x}=0.75$
0.375+x=0.45-0.75x
1.75x=0.075; x≈0.0429
New [A]=0.6-0.0429=0.557 M; [B]=0.375+0.0429=0.418 M

</details>

### Q10. JEE Main 2026 (23 January Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

For the following gas phase equilibrium reaction at constant temperature, 
 $\mathrm{NH}_{3}(\mathrm{~g}) \rightleftharpoons 1 / 2 \mathrm{~N}_{2}(\mathrm{~g})+3 / 2 \mathrm{H}_{2}(\mathrm{~g})$ 
 if the total pressure is $\sqrt{3} \mathrm{~atm}$ and the pressure equilibrium constant $\left(\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{p}}\right)$ is 9 atm, then the degree of dissociation is given as $\left(x \times 10^{-2}\right)^{-1 / 2}$. The value of $x$ is $\_\_\_\_$. (nearest integer)

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **125**

**Detailed Solution:**

For the reaction: $NH_3(g) \rightleftharpoons \frac{1}{2}N_2(g) + \frac{3}{2}H_2(g)$

At $t = 0$: 1 mole, –, –
At equilibrium: $1-\alpha$, $\frac{\alpha}{2}$, $\frac{3\alpha}{2}$
Total moles $= 1 + \alpha$

$K_P = \frac{\left(\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)^{1/2}\left(\frac{3\alpha}{2}\right)^{3/2}}{(1-\alpha)} \cdot \left(\frac{P_T}{1+\alpha}\right)^1$

Given $P_T = \sqrt{3}$ atm and $K_P = 9$ atm:

$9 = \frac{\left(\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)^{1/2}\left(\frac{3\alpha}{2}\right)^{3/2}}{(1-\alpha)} \times \frac{(3)^{1/2}}{1+\alpha}$

Simplifying: $9 = \frac{9\left(\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)^2}{1-\alpha^2}$

$1 - \alpha^2 = \frac{\alpha^2}{4}$

$\frac{5\alpha^2}{4} = 1 \Rightarrow \alpha^2 = 0.8$

$\alpha = (0.8)^{1/2} = \left(\frac{1}{0.8}\right)^{-1/2} = (125 \times 10^{-2})^{-1/2}$

So $x = 125$.

</details>

---

### Q11. JEE Main 2026 (23 January Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Consider the general reaction given below at 400 K 
 $x \mathrm{~A}(\mathrm{~g}) \rightleftharpoons y \mathrm{~B}(\mathrm{~g})$ 
 The values of $\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{p}}$ and $\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{c}}$ are studied under the same condition of temperature but variation in $x$ and $y$. 
 (i) $\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{p}}=85.87$ and $\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{c}}=2.586$ appropriate units 
 (ii) $\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{p}}=0.862$ and $\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{c}}=28.62$ appropriate units. 
 The values of $x$ and $y$ in (i) and (ii) respectively are:

**Options:**

- **(A)** \(\begin{array}{c} \text {(i)} & \text {(ii)} \\ 1,3 & 2,1 \end{array}\)
- **(B)** \(\begin{array}{c} \text {(i)} & \text {(ii)} \\ 4,1 & 4,1 \end{array}\)
- **(C)** \(\begin{array}{c} \text {(i)} & \text {(ii)} \\ 3,1 & 3,1 \end{array}\)
- **(D)** \(\begin{array}{c} \text {(i)} & \text {(ii)} \\ 1,2 & 2,1 \end{array}\)

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) \(\begin{array}{c} \text {(i)} & \text {(ii)} \\ 1,2 & 2,1 \end{array}\)**

**Detailed Solution:**

The relationship between $K_p$ and $K_c$ is given by $K_p = K_c(RT)^{\Delta n_g}$, where $\Delta n_g = y - x$.
Given $T = 400$ K and $R = 0.0821$ L atm K$^{-1}$ mol$^{-1}$.
The value of $RT = 0.0821 \times 400 = 32.84$.
For case (i): $K_p = 85.87$ and $K_c = 2.586$.
$\frac{K_p}{K_c} = \frac{85.87}{2.586} \approx 33.2$.
Since $33.2 \approx 32.84^1$, we have $\Delta n_g = y - x = 1$.
Checking options for (i):
(1) $y-x = 3-1 = 2$
(2) $y-x = 1-4 = -3$
(3) $y-x = 1-3 = -2$
(4) $y-x = 2-1 = 1$. This matches.
For case (ii): $K_p = 0.862$ and $K_c = 28.62$.
$\frac{K_p}{K_c} = \frac{0.862}{28.62} \approx 0.0301$.
Since $0.0301 \approx \frac{1}{32.84} = 32.84^{-1}$, we have $\Delta n_g = y - x = -1$.
Checking option (4) for (ii): $y-x = 1-2 = -1$. This also matches.
Thus, for (i) $x=1, y=2$ and for (ii) $x=2, y=1$.

</details>

---

### Q12. JEE Main 2026 (22 January Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

Dissociation of a gas $\mathrm{A}_{2}$ takes place according to the following chemical reaction. At equilibrium, the total pressure is 1 bar at 300 K. 
 $\mathrm{A}_{2}(\mathrm{~g}) \rightleftharpoons 2 \mathrm{~A}(\mathrm{~g})$ 
 The standard Gibbs energy of formation of the involved substances has been provided below: 
 \(\begin{array}{|c|c|} \hline \text { Substance } & \Delta \mathrm{G}_{\mathrm{f}}^{\circ} / \mathrm{kJ} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1} \\ \hline \mathrm{~A}_2 & -100.00 \\ \hline \mathrm{~A} & -50.832 \\ \hline \end{array}\) 
 The degree of dissociation of $\mathrm{A}_{2}(\mathrm{~g})$ is given by $\left(x \times 10^{-2}\right)^{1 / 2}$ where $x=$ $\_\_\_\_$. (Nearest integer). 
 [Given: $\mathrm{R}=8 \mathrm{~J} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1} \mathrm{~K}^{-1}, \log 2=0.3010, \log 3=0.48$ ] 
 Assume degree of dissociation is not negligible.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **33**

**Detailed Solution:**

For the dissociation reaction $A_2(g) \rightleftharpoons 2A(g)$, first calculate the standard Gibbs free energy:
$\Delta G°_{rxn} = 2(-50.832) - (-100.00) = -1.664$ kJ/mol.
Using $\Delta G° = -RT \ln K_p$:
$-1664 = -8 \times 300 \times \ln K_p$, giving $K_p = e^{0.6933} = 2$.
For dissociation with degree of dissociation $\alpha$, starting with 1 mole of $A_2$ at 1 bar total pressure:
At equilibrium, moles are $(1-\alpha)$ for $A_2$ and $2\alpha$ for $A$, with total $(1+\alpha)$ moles.
$K_p = \frac{4\alpha^2}{1-\alpha^2} = 2$.
Solving: $2(1-\alpha^2) = 4\alpha^2$ gives $\alpha^2 = 1/3$ or $\alpha = 0.577$.
Since degree of dissociation is $(x \times 10^{-2})^{1/2}$:
$(x \times 10^{-2})^{1/2} = 0.577$ gives $x \times 10^{-2} = 0.333$, so $x = 33.3 \approx 33$.

</details>

### Q13. JEE Main 2026 (21 January Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

For the reaction, $\mathrm{N}_{2} \mathrm{O}_{4} \rightleftharpoons 2 \mathrm{NO}_{2}$, graph is plotted as shown below. Identify correct statements.
A. Standard free energy change for the reaction is $-5.40 \mathrm{~kJ} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$.
B. As $\Delta \mathrm{G}^{\ominus}$ in graph is positive, $\mathrm{N}_{2} \mathrm{O}_{4}$ will not dissociate into $\mathrm{NO}_{2}$ at all.
C. Reverse reaction will go to completion.
D. When 1 mole of $\mathrm{N}_{2} \mathrm{O}_{4}$ changes into equilibrium mixture, value of $\Delta \mathrm{G}^{\ominus}=-0.84 \mathrm{~kJ} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
E. When 2 mole of $\mathrm{NO}_{2}$ changes into equilibrium mixture, $\Delta \mathrm{G}^{\ominus}$ for equilibrium mixture is $-6.24 \mathrm{~kJ} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$. 
 <img style="width:400px;" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main_2026_watermark_improved/21S1_q_65_1.png"> 
 Choose the correct answer from the options given below :

**Options:**

- **(A)** D and E only
- **(B)** A and D only
- **(C)** B and C only
- **(D)** C and E only

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) D and E only**

**Detailed Solution:**

From the graph of G vs fraction of $N_2O_4$ dissociated:
At $x = 0$ (pure $N_2O_4$): $G = 0$
At equilibrium ($x \approx 0.4$): $G = -0.84$ kJ/mol (minimum)
At $x = 1$ (complete dissociation): $G = 5.40$ kJ/mol
Statement A: $\Delta G° = 5.40$ kJ/mol (positive), not $-5.40$ kJ/mol. False.
Statement B: Even with positive $\Delta G°$, equilibrium is established with partial dissociation. False.
Statement C: Reverse reaction reaches equilibrium, not completion. False.
Statement D: $\Delta G = G_{eq} - G_{N_2O_4} = -0.84 - 0 = -0.84$ kJ/mol. True.
Statement E: $\Delta G = G_{eq} - G_{NO_2} = -0.84 - 5.40 = -6.24$ kJ/mol. True.

</details>

### Q14. JEE Main 2025 (2 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

Consider the following chemical equilibrium of the gas phase reaction at a constant temperature :
$\mathrm{A}(\mathrm{~g}) \rightleftharpoons \mathrm{B}(\mathrm{~g})+\mathrm{C}(\mathrm{~g})$
If $p$ being the total pressure, $K_p$ is the pressure equilibrium constant and $\alpha$ is the degree of dissociation, then which of the following is true at equilibrium?

**Options:**

- **(A)** If p value is extremely high compared to $\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{p}}, \alpha \approx 1$
- **(B)** When $p$ increases $\alpha$ decreases
- **(C)** If $k_p$ value is extremely high compared to $p, \alpha$ becomes much less than unity
- **(D)** When $p$ increases $\alpha$ increases

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) When $p$ increases $\alpha$ decreases**

**Detailed Solution:**

<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/jO-uByipsA3pMQdmHtpS2Z1z-DMtUpr02f3h6ZUTp6g.original.fullsize.png"/>
a moles of $A(g)$ taken initially and at time Now moles fraction of $\mathrm{A}(\mathrm{g}), \mathrm{B}(\mathrm{g})$ and $\mathrm{C}(\mathrm{g})$ are
$\begin{aligned}
& X_A=\frac{a-a \alpha}{a+a \alpha}=\frac{1-\alpha}{1+\alpha} \\
& X_B=\frac{a \alpha}{a+a \alpha}=\frac{\alpha}{1+\alpha} \\
& X_C=\frac{a \alpha}{a+a \alpha}=\frac{\alpha}{1+\alpha}
\end{aligned}$
Now if $P$ is total pressure then partial pressure o $\mathrm{A}(\mathrm{g}), \mathrm{B}(\mathrm{g})$ and $\mathrm{C}(\mathrm{g})$ are
$\begin{aligned}
& \mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{A}}=\left(\frac{1-\alpha}{1+\alpha}\right) \mathrm{P} \\
& \mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{B}}=\left(\frac{\alpha}{1+\alpha}\right) \mathrm{P} \\
& \mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{C}}=\left(\frac{\alpha}{1+\alpha}\right) \mathrm{P} \\
& \mathrm{~K}_{\mathrm{P}}=\frac{\left(\frac{\alpha}{1+\alpha}\right) \mathrm{P}\left(\frac{\alpha}{1+\alpha}\right) \mathrm{P}}{\left(\frac{1-\alpha}{1+\alpha}\right) \mathrm{P}} \\
& \mathrm{~K}_{\mathrm{P}}=\frac{\alpha^2 \mathrm{P}}{1-\alpha^2}
\end{aligned}$
As $K_P$ is only function of temperature.
So as P $\uparrow \quad \alpha \downarrow$

</details>

### Q15. JEE Main 2025 (2 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Consider the following equilibrium,
\(\mathrm{CO}(\mathrm{g})+2 \mathrm{H}_2(\mathrm{g}) \rightleftharpoons \mathrm{CH}_3 \mathrm{OH}(\mathrm{g})\)
0.1 mol of CO along with a catalyst is present in a \(2 \mathrm{dm}^3\) flask maintained at 500 K. Hydrogen is introduced into the flask until the pressure is 5 bar and 0.04 mol of \(\mathrm{CH}_3 \mathrm{OH}\) is formed. The \(\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{p}}^0\) is ______ \(\times 10^{-3}\) (nearest integer).
Given : \(\mathrm{R}=0.08 \mathrm{dm}^3\) bar \(\mathrm{K}^{-1} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}\)
Assume only methanol is formed as the product and the system follows ideal gas behaviour.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **74**

**Detailed Solution:**

\(\begin{array}{llll} 
& \mathrm{CO}(\mathrm{g}) & + & 2 \mathrm{H}_2(\mathrm{g}) & \rightleftharpoons & \mathrm{CH}_3 \mathrm{OH}(\mathrm{g}) \\
\mathrm{t}=0 & 0.1 \mathrm{~mol} & & \mathrm{a} \mathrm{~mol} & & - \\
\mathrm{t}_{\text {eq }} & 0.1-\mathrm{x} & & \mathrm{a}-2 \mathrm{x} & & \mathrm{x}=0.04 \\
& =0.06 & & =\mathrm{a}-0.08 & \\
& & & =0.23-0.08 \\
& & & =0.15 \mathrm{~mole}
\end{array}\)
\(\begin{aligned} & \mathrm{V}=2 \mathrm{~L} \\ & \mathrm{~T}=500 \mathrm{~K} \\ & \mathrm{P}_{\text {total }}=5 \mathrm{bar} \\ & \mathrm{n}_{\text {Total }}=0.25=\frac{1}{4} \mathrm{~mol} \\ & \mathrm{P}_{\text {total }}=\mathrm{n}_{\text {total }} \times \frac{\mathrm{RT}}{\mathrm{V}} \\ & \Rightarrow 5=(0.06+\mathrm{a}-0.08+0.04) \times \frac{0.08 \times 500}{2} \\ & \Rightarrow 10=(0.02+\mathrm{a}) \times 0.08 \times 500\end{aligned}\)
\(\Rightarrow \mathrm{a}=0.25-0.02=0.23 \mathrm{~mol}.\)
\(\begin{aligned}
& \mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{P}}=\frac{\mathrm{X}_{\mathrm{CH}_3 \mathrm{OH}}}{\mathrm{X}_{\mathrm{CO}} \times \mathrm{X}_{\mathrm{H}_2}^2} \times \frac{1}{\left(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{T}}\right)^2}=\frac{0.04}{0.06 \times(0.15)^2} \times\left[\frac{1 / 4}{5}\right]^2 \\
& =\frac{4}{6 \times(0.15)^2 \times 16} \times \frac{1}{25} \\
& =\frac{100 \times 100}{24 \times 225 \times 25}=\frac{100 \times 100}{135000} \\
& =0.074=74 \times 10^{-3}
\end{aligned}\)

</details>

---

### Q16. JEE Main 2025 (29 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Consider the equilibrium
$\mathrm{CO}(\mathrm{~g})+3 \mathrm{H}_2(\mathrm{~g}) \rightleftharpoons \mathrm{CH}_4(\mathrm{~g})+\mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{O}(\mathrm{~g})$

If the pressure applied over the system increases by two fold at constant temperature then
(A) Concentration of reactants and products increases.
(B) Equilibrium will shift in forward direction.
(C) Equilibrium constant increases since concentration of products increases.
(D) Equilibrium constant remains unchanged as concentration of reactants and products remain same.
Choose the correct answer from the options given below :

**Options:**

- **(A)** (A), (B) and (C) only
- **(B)** (A) and (B) only
- **(C)** (A), (B) and (D) only
- **(D)** (B) and (C) only

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) (A) and (B) only**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{aligned} & \mathrm{CO}(\mathrm{g})+3 \mathrm{H}_2 \rightleftharpoons \mathrm{CH}_4(\mathrm{~g})+\mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{O}(\mathrm{g}) \\ & \Delta \mathrm{n}_{\mathrm{g}}=-2\end{aligned}$
If pressure of system increases then according to Le-Chatelier's principle reaction will move in forward direction.
Concentration of reactant and products both increases but concentration of product increases more.

</details>

### Q17. JEE Main 2025 (29 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

At temperature T, compound \(\mathrm{AB}_{2(\mathrm{~g})}\) dissociates as \(\mathrm{AB}_{2(\mathrm{~g})} \rightleftharpoons \mathrm{AB}_{(\mathrm{g})}+\frac{1}{2} \mathrm{~B}_{2(\mathrm{~g})}\) having degree of dissociation \(x\) (small compared to unity). The correct expression for \(x\) in terms of \(\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{p}}\) and p is

**Options:**

- **(A)** \(\sqrt[4]{\frac{2 K_p}{p}}\)
- **(B)** \(\sqrt[3]{\frac{2 K_{\mathrm{p}}}{\mathrm{p}}}\)
- **(C)** \(\sqrt[3]{\frac{2 \mathrm{~K}_{\mathrm{p}}^2}{\mathrm{p}}}\)
- **(D)** \(\sqrt{K_p}\)

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) \(\sqrt[3]{\frac{2 \mathrm{~K}_{\mathrm{p}}^2}{\mathrm{p}}}\)**

**Detailed Solution:**

\(\begin{array}{lcc}
& \mathrm{AB}_2(\mathrm{g}) & \rightleftharpoons & \mathrm{AB}(\mathrm{g}) & + & \frac{1}{2} \mathrm{~B}_2(\mathrm{g}) \\
t=0 & p_0 & \\
t=t_{\text {eq }} & p_0(1-x) & & p_0 x & & \frac{p_0 x}{2}
\end{array}\)
\(\mathrm{p}=\mathrm{p}_0 \mathrm{x}-\mathrm{p}_0 \mathrm{x}+\mathrm{p}_0 \mathrm{x}+\frac{\mathrm{p}_0 \mathrm{x}}{2}\)
$\begin{gathered}p=p_0\left(1+\frac{x}{2}\right) \\ p_0=\frac{p}{\left(1+\frac{x}{2}\right)} \\ K_p=\frac{\left(p_{A B}\right)\left(p_{B_2}\right)^{1 / 2}}{\left(p_{\mathrm{AB}_2}\right)} \\ K_p=\frac{\left(p_0 x\right)\left(\frac{p_0 x}{2}\right)^{1 / 2}}{p_0(1-x)}\end{gathered}$
$\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{p}}=\frac{\frac{\mathrm{px}}{\left(1+\frac{x}{2}\right)}\left(\frac{\mathrm{p}}{1+\frac{x}{2}} \times \frac{\mathrm{x}}{2}\right)^{1 / 2}}{\frac{\mathrm{p}(1-\mathrm{x})}{\left(1+\frac{x}{2}\right)}}$
Since $x \ll 1$
$\begin{aligned}
& K_p=\frac{\mathrm{p}^{1 / 2} \mathrm{x}^{3 / 2}}{2^{1 / 2}} \\
& \mathrm{x}=\sqrt[3]{\frac{2 \mathrm{~K}_{\mathrm{p}}^2}{\mathrm{p}}}
\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q19. JEE Main 2025 (24 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

$37.8 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~N}_2 \mathrm{O}_5$ was taken in a 1 L reaction vessel and allowed to undergo the following reaction at 500 K
$2 \mathrm{~N}_2 \mathrm{O}_{5(\mathrm{~g})} \rightleftharpoons 2 \mathrm{~N}_2 \mathrm{O}_{4(\mathrm{~g})}+\mathrm{O}_{2(\mathrm{~g})}$
The total pressure at equilibrium was found to be 18.65 bar.
Then, $\mathrm{Kp}=$ ______ $\times 10^{-2}$ [nearest integer]
Assume $\mathrm{N}_2 \mathrm{O}_5$ to behave ideally under these conditions.
Given: $\mathrm{R}=0.082$ bar $\mathrm{L} \mathrm{mol}^{-1} \mathrm{~K}^{-1}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **962**

**Detailed Solution:**

Initial pressure of $\mathrm{N}_2 \mathrm{O}_5$
$\begin{aligned}
& =\frac{\frac{37.8}{108} \times 0.082 \times 500}{1}=14.35 \text { bar } \\
& 2 \mathrm{~N}_2 \mathrm{O}_5 \rightleftharpoons 2 \mathrm{~N}_2 \mathrm{O}_4+\mathrm{O}_2
\end{aligned}$
$\begin{array}{lll}\mathrm{t}=0 & 14.35 \\ \mathrm{t}=\mathrm{eq} & 14.35-2 \mathrm{P} & 2 \mathrm{P}\end{array}$
$\begin{aligned} & \mathrm{P}_{\text {Total }} \text { at eqb }=14.35+\mathrm{P}=18.65 \\ & \mathrm{P}=4.3 \\ & \mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{N}_2 \mathrm{O}_5}=5.75 \text { bar } \\ & \mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{N}_2 \mathrm{O}_4}=8.6 \mathrm{bar} \\ & \mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{O}_2}=4.3 \text { bar } \\ & \mathrm{k}_{\mathrm{p}}=\frac{(8.6)^2 \times(4.3)}{(5.75)^2}=9.619=\mathrm{x} \times 10^{-2} \\ & \mathrm{x}=961.9 \approx 962\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q20. JEE Main 2025 (23 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Consider the reaction
$\mathrm{X}_2 \mathrm{Y}(\mathrm{~g})=\mathrm{X}_2(\mathrm{~g})+\frac{1}{2} \mathrm{Y}_2(\mathrm{~g})$
The equation representing correct relationship between the degree of dissociation (x) of $\mathrm{X}_2 \mathrm{Y}(\mathrm{g})$ with its equilibrium constant Kp is ______ .
Assume $x$ to be very very small.

**Options:**

- **(A)** $x=\sqrt[3]{\frac{2 \mathrm{Kp}}{\mathrm{p}}}$
- **(B)** $x=\sqrt[3]{\frac{2 \mathrm{Kp}^2}{\mathrm{p}}}$
- **(C)** $x=\sqrt[3]{\frac{\mathrm{Kp}}{\mathrm{p}}}$
- **(D)** $x=\sqrt[3]{\frac{K \mathrm{p}}{2 \mathrm{p}}}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $x=\sqrt[3]{\frac{2 \mathrm{Kp}^2}{\mathrm{p}}}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{aligned} & 1 \mathrm{~mol} \\ & \mathrm{X}_2 \mathrm{Y}(\mathrm{g}) \rightarrow \underset{\mathrm{x}}{\mathrm{X}} \mathrm{X}_2(\mathrm{~g})+\frac{1}{2} \mathrm{Y}_2(\mathrm{~g}) \\ & \frac{\mathrm{x}}{2} \\ & \therefore \quad P_{\mathrm{x}_2 \mathrm{y}} \\ & =\frac{1-\mathrm{x}}{1+\frac{x}{2}} \times \mathrm{p} \\ & P_{x_2}=\frac{x}{1+\frac{x}{2}} \times p\end{aligned}$
$\begin{aligned}
& P_{y_2}=\frac{x / 2}{1+\frac{x}{2}} \times p \\
\therefore \quad K_p= & \frac{\left(\frac{x}{1+\frac{x}{2}} p\right)\left(\frac{x}{2\left(1+\frac{x}{2}\right)} p\right)^{1 / 2}}{\left(\frac{1-x}{1+\frac{x}{2}}\right) \times p} \\
\therefore \quad & K_p=\left(\frac{x}{1-x}\right)\left(\frac{x}{2\left(1+\frac{x}{2}\right)}\right)^{1 / 2} \times p^{1 / 2}
\end{aligned}$
$\therefore \quad \mathrm{x}$ to be very small
$\begin{aligned}
\therefore & K_p=\frac{x^{3 / 2}}{2^{(1 / 2)}} \times p^{1 / 2} \\
\therefore & x^{3 / 2}=\frac{K_p \times 2^{1 / 2}}{p^{1 / 2}} \\
& x^3=\frac{K_p^2 \times 2}{p} \\
& x=\left(\frac{K_p^2 \times 2}{p}\right)^{1 / 3}
\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q22. JEE Main 2024 (08 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

For the given hypothetical reactions, the equilibrium constants are as follows :
\(\begin{aligned}
& \mathrm{X} \rightleftharpoons \mathrm{Y} ; \mathrm{K}_1=1.0 \\
& \mathrm{Y} \rightleftharpoons \mathrm{Z} ; \mathrm{K}_2=2.0 \\
& \mathrm{Z} \rightleftharpoons \mathrm{W} ; \mathrm{K}_3=4.0
\end{aligned}\)
The equilibrium constant for the reaction \(\mathrm{X} \rightleftharpoons \mathrm{W}\) is

**Options:**

- **(A)** 6.0
- **(B)** 12.0
- **(C)** 7.0
- **(D)** 8.0

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) 8.0**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{array}{ll}
\mathrm{X} \rightleftharpoons \mathrm{Y} & \mathrm{k}_1=1 \\
\mathrm{Y} \rightleftharpoons \mathrm{Z} & \mathrm{k}_2=2 \\
\mathrm{Z} \rightleftharpoons \omega & \mathrm{k}_3=4 \\
\mathrm{X} \rightleftharpoons \omega & \mathrm{k}_1 \cdot \mathrm{k}_2 \cdot \mathrm{k}_3 \\
& \mathrm{k}=1 \times 2 \times 4 \\
& \mathrm{k}=8
\end{array}$

</details>

### Q23. JEE Main 2024 (06 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

The ratio \(\frac{K_P}{K_C}\) for the reaction : \(\mathrm{CO}_{(\mathrm{g})}+\frac{1}{2} \mathrm{O}_{2(\mathrm{~g})} \rightleftharpoons \mathrm{CO}_{2(\mathrm{~g})}\) is :

**Options:**

- **(A)** \(\frac{1}{\sqrt{\mathrm{RT}}}\)
- **(B)** \((\mathrm{RT})^{1 / 2}\)
- **(C)** \(\mathrm{RT}\)
- **(D)** 1

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) \(\frac{1}{\sqrt{\mathrm{RT}}}\)**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{aligned} & \mathrm{CO}(\mathrm{g})+\frac{1}{2} \mathrm{O}_2(\mathrm{~g}) \rightleftharpoons \mathrm{CO}_2(\mathrm{~g}) \\ & \Delta \mathrm{n}_{\mathrm{g}}=1-\left(1+\frac{1}{2}\right)=-\frac{1}{2} \\ & \frac{\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{p}}}{\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{C}}}=(\mathrm{RT})^{\Delta \mathrm{n}_{\mathrm{g}}}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\mathrm{RT}}}\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q24. JEE Main 2024 (06 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

At \(-20^{\circ} \mathrm{C}\) and \(1 \mathrm{~atm}\) pressure, a cylinder is filled with equal number of \(\mathrm{H}_2, \mathrm{I}_2\) and \(\mathrm{HI}\) molecules for the reaction
\(\mathrm{H}_2(\mathrm{~g})+\mathrm{I}_2(\mathrm{~g}) \rightleftharpoons 2 \mathrm{HI}(\mathrm{g})\), the \(\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{p}}\) for the process is \(x \times 10^{-1}\).
\(\mathrm{x}=\) _____
[Given : \(\mathrm{R}=0.082 \mathrm{~L} \mathrm{~atm} \mathrm{~K}^{-1} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}\)]

- **(A)** 0.01
- **(B)** 10
- **(C)** 2
- **(D)** 1

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) 10**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{array}{ll}\Delta \mathrm{n}_{\mathrm{g}}=0 & \mathrm{~K}_{\mathrm{p}}=\frac{\left(\mathrm{n}_{\mathrm{HI}}\right)^2}{\mathrm{n}_{\mathrm{H}_2} \mathrm{n}_{\mathrm{I}_2}}\left(\frac{\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{T}}}{\mathrm{n}_{\mathrm{T}}}\right)^{\Delta \mathrm{n}_{\mathrm{g}}} \\ \mathrm{n}_{\mathrm{HI}}=\mathrm{n}_{\mathrm{H}_2}=\mathrm{n}_{\mathrm{I}_2} & \text { so } \mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{P}}=1 \\ 1=\mathrm{x} \times 10^{-1} & \mathrm{x}=10\end{array}$

### Q26. JEE Main 2024 (31 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>A</mi><mfenced><mi>g</mi></mfenced></msub><mo>⇌</mo><msub><mi>B</mi><mfenced><mi>g</mi></mfenced></msub><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mi>C</mi><msup><mn>2</mn><mfenced><mi>g</mi></mfenced></msup></mfrac></math>. **Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>K</mi><mi>C</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>F</mi><mi>e</mi><mi>S</mi><mi>C</mi><msup><mi>N</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>F</mi><msup><mi>e</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>S</mi><mi>C</mi><msup><mi>N</mi><mo>-</mo></msup></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>K</mi><mi>C</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>F</mi><msup><mi>e</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>S</mi><mi>C</mi><msup><mi>N</mi><mo>-</mo></msup></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>F</mi><mi>e</mi><mi>S</mi><mi>C</mi><msup><mi>N</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced></mfrac></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>K</mi><mi>C</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>F</mi><mi>e</mi><mi>S</mi><mi>C</mi><msup><mi>N</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced><mrow><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>F</mi><msup><mi>e</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>S</mi><mi>C</mi><msup><mi>N</mi><mo>-</mo></msup></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>K</mi><mi>C</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>F</mi><mi>e</mi><mi>S</mi><mi>C</mi><msup><mi>N</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup><mrow><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>F</mi><msup><mi>e</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>S</mi><mi>C</mi><msup><mi>N</mi><mo>-</mo></msup></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mfrac></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>K</mi><mi>C</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>F</mi><mi>e</mi><mi>S</mi><mi>C</mi><msup><mi>N</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>F</mi><msup><mi>e</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>S</mi><mi>C</mi><msup><mi>N</mi><mo>-</mo></msup></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mfrac></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>The equilibrium constant of a chemical reaction (usually denoted by the symbol K) provides insight into the relationship between the products and reactants when a chemical reaction reaches equilibrium. For example, the equilibrium constant of concentration (denoted by K<sub>c</sub>) of a chemical reaction at equilibrium can be defined as the ratio of the concentration of products to the concentration of the reactants, each raised to their respective stoichiometric coefficients. It is important to note that there are different types of equilibrium constants that provide relationships between the products and the reactants of equilibrium reactions in terms of different units.</p><p>Equilibrium Constant Formula</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>K</mi><mi>eqb</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">b</mi></msub></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi></msup><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mi mathvariant="normal">D</mi></mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi></msup></mrow><mrow><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi></msup><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">b</mi></msup></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi></msub></math></p><p>Where <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi></msub></math> indicates the equilibrium constant measured in moles per litre.</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>K</mi><mi>C</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mfenced close="]" open="["><mi>Products</mi></mfenced><mo> </mo></mrow><mrow><mfenced close="]" open="["><mi>Reactants</mi></mfenced><mo> </mo></mrow></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mfenced close="]" open="["><msup><mi>FeSCN</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></mfenced><mrow><mfenced close="]" open="["><msup><mi>Fe</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></mfenced><mfenced close="]" open="["><msup><mi>SCN</mi><mo>-</mo></msup></mfenced></mrow></mfrac></math></p><p>Hence, the answer is option A.</p>

</details>

### Q29. JEE Main 2024 (29 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

For the reaction <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>4</mn></msub><mo>(</mo><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>⇌</mo><mn>2</mn><msub><mi>NO</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>(</mo><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>)</mo></math>, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">p</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>492</mn><mo> </mo><mi>atm</mi></math> at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>300</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mo>.</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi></msub></math> for the reaction at same temperature is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>_</mi><mi>_</mi><mi>_</mi><mi>_</mi><mi>_</mi><mi>_</mi><mo>×</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>. (Given :<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>082</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi><mo> </mo><mi>atm</mi><mo> </mo><msup><mi>mol</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>)

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **2**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>The given reaction is,</p><p> <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>4</mn></msub><mo>(</mo><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>⇌</mo><mn>2</mn><msub><mi>NO</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>(</mo><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>)</mo></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">p</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>492</mn><mo> </mo><mi>atm</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>300</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></msub><mo>·</mo><mo>(</mo><mi>RT</mi><msup><mo>)</mo><msub><mi>Δn</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></msub></msup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>Δn</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></msub><mo>=</mo></math> number of gaseous molecules of products – number of gaseous molecules of reactants.</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>Δn</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></math></p><p>By putting the values in the above equation,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>⇒</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">p</mi></msub><mi>RT</mi></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>492</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>082</mn><mo>×</mo><mn>300</mn></mrow></mfrac><mspace linebreak="newline"></mspace><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math></p>

</details>

### Q30. JEE Main 2023 (11 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn></math> moles each of hydrogen and iodine is heated in a sealed ten litre vessel.At equilibrium, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn></math> moles of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>HI</mi></math> were found. The equilibrium constant for <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mfenced><mo>+</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mfenced><mo>&#8652;</mo><mn>2</mn><msub><mi>HI</mi><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mfenced></msub></math> is ........</p>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **1**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>The equilibrium moles of each reactant and product can be calculated as follows,</p><p> <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mfenced><mo>+</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mfenced><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#8652;</mo><mn>2</mn><msub><mi>HI</mi><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mfenced></msub><mspace linebreak="newline"></mspace><mi>initial</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>0</mn><mspace linebreak="newline"></mspace><mi>At</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>equilibrium</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>3</mn></math></p><p>Now, using equilibrium moles, equilibrium constant calculated as shown below.
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mi>eq</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mo>[</mo><mi>HI</mi><msup><mo>]</mo><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow><mrow><mfenced open="[" close="]"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mfenced><mfenced open="[" close="]"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mfenced></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mo>(</mo><mn>3</mn><msup><mo>)</mo><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></math></p>

### Q32. JEE Main 2023 (10 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

<p>The number of correct statement/s involving equilibria in physical processes from the following is ________</p><p>(A) Equilibrium is possible only in a closed system at a given temperature.</p><p>(B) Both the opposing processes occur at the same rate.</p><p>(C) When equilibrium is attained at a given temperature, the value of all its parameters became equal</p><p>(D) For dissolution of solids in liquids, the solubility is constant at a given temperature.</p>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **3**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>The equilibrium is possible when there is no exchange of matter between system and surroundings.</p><p>At equilibrium state the forward and backward processes occur with same rate (speed)</p><p>When a system reaches equilibrium at a given temperature, the forward and reverse reactions are occurring at the same rate, and the concentrations (or partial pressures) of the reactants and products no longer change with time. At this point, the system has achieved a steady state, where the rate of the forward reaction is equal to the rate of the reverse reaction. So when equilibrium is attained, the value of all its parameters became constant.</p><p>The solubility is function of solubility product, the value of which is constant at a given temperature.</p></blockquote>

</details>

### Q34. JEE Main 2023 (06 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

<p>For a concentrated solution of a weak electrolyte (<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mi>eq</mi></msub><mo>=</mo></math>equilibrium constant) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></math> of concentration <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8216;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>&#8217;</mo><mo>,</mo></math> the degree of dissociation <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8216;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#945;</mi><mo>&#8217;</mo></math> is</p>

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced><mfrac><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mi>eq</mi></msub><mrow><mn>5</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi><mn>4</mn></msup></mrow></mfrac></mfenced><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>5</mn></mfrac></msup></math>

- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced><mfrac><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mi>eq</mi></msub><mrow><mn>25</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow></mfrac></mfenced><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>5</mn></mfrac></msup></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced><mfrac><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mi>eq</mi></msub><mrow><mn>6</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi><mn>5</mn></msup></mrow></mfrac></mfenced><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>5</mn></mfrac></msup></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced><mfrac><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mi>eq</mi></msub><mrow><mn>108</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi><mn>4</mn></msup></mrow></mfrac></mfenced><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>5</mn></mfrac></msup></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>Given that concentration of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></math> and degree of dissociation is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#945;</mi></math></p><p>The equilibrium reaction of weak electrolyte <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></math> is</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mtable><mtr><mtd><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></mtd><mtd><mo>-</mo></mtd><mtd><mo>-</mo></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd><mo>&#8595;</mo></mtd><mtd><mo>&#8595;</mo></mtd><mtd><mo>&#8595;</mo></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mi>&#945;</mi></mtd></mtr></mtable></math></p><p>Now, The equilibrium constant is the ratio of the concentrations of reactants and products raised to the stoichiometric coefficients.</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>(</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#945;</mi><mo>)</mo></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>108</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>4</mn></msup><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">&#945;</mi><mn>5</mn></msup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mi>eq</mi></msub><mrow><mn>108</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>4</mn></msup></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><msup><mi ### Q35. JEE Main 2023 (01 Feb Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

<p>(i) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">X</mi><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>&#8652;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">Y</mi><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>+</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">Z</mi><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">p</mi><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn></math></p><p>(ii) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>&#8652;</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>(</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>&#160;&#160;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">p</mi><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></math></p><p>If the degree of dissociation and initial concentration of both the reactants <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">X</mi><mo>(</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>)</mo></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>)</mo></math> are equal, then the ratio of the total pressure at equilibrium <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mfrac><msub><mi>p</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><msub><mi>p</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mfrac></mfenced></math> is equal to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo>:</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>1</mn></math>. The value of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi></math> is (Nearest integer)</p>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **12**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">X</mi><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#8652;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">Y</mi><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>+</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">Z</mi><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>&#160;&#160;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">p</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn></math></p><p> <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi></math>                                   Initial concentration</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>&#945;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mo>&#160;&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>&#945;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mo>&#160;&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>&#945;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi></math>     at equilibrium
Let total pressure be <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">p</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></math></p><p>Using dalton&#39;s law of partial pressures and equation for equilibrium constant:
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8756;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">p</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><msup><mfenced><mrow><mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">&#945;</mi><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#945;</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">p</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#945;</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#945;</mi></mrow></mfrac><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">p</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></mrow></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">&#945;</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>&#215;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">p</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">&#945;</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow></mfrac></math>       ....(X)</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>&#8652;</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>(</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>&#160;&#160;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">p</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></math></p><p> <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mo>&#160;</mo></math>                         Initial mole</p><p> <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>&#945;n</mi><mo>&#160;&#160;</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>&#945;n</mi><mo>&#160;&#160;</mo></math>   at equilibrium
Let total pressure be <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">p</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math>
Using dalton&#39;s law of partial pressures and equation for equilibrium constant</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8756;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">p</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><msup><mfenced><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>&#945;</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>&#945;</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">p</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mi>&#945;</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>&#945;</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">p</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>4</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">&#945;</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>&#215;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">p</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">&#945;</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow></mfrac></math>   ....(Y)
Thus, dividing equation (X) by equation(Y)</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mfrac><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">p</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">p</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></msub></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">p</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mrow><mn>4</mn><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">p</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mfrac><mn>3</mn><mn>1</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">p</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mrow><mn>4</mn><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">p</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8756;</mo><mfrac><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">p</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">p</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>12</mn></math></p><p> </p>

</details>

mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mn>5</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>(</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi **Detailed Solution:**

<p>Given,

Using properties of equilibrium constant:</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mn>2</mn></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mfenced><mn>3</mn></mfenced><mo>-</mo><mfenced><mn>1</mn></mfenced></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mn>5</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mfenced><mo>&#8652;</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>NO</mi><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mfenced></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mi>eq</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>&#215;</mo><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mn>3</mn><mn>3</mn></msubsup></mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>6</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>12</mn></msup><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mfenced><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>13</mn></mrow></msup></mfenced><mn>3</mn></msup></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>5</mn></msup></mrow></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>6</mn></mrow><mn>4</mn></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>32</mn></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>33</mn></mrow></msup></math></p>

### Q38. JEE Main 2023 (29 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

<p>Water decomposes at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2300</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mfenced><mo>&#8594;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mfenced></math></p><p>The percent of water decomposing at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2300</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></math> and 1 bar is _____ (Nearest integer). Equilibrium constant for the reaction is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup></math> at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2300</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></math></p>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **2**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Decomposition of water gives:
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mfenced><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#8652;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mfenced><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>+</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mfenced></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#945;</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#160;&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">&#945;</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">&#945;</mi></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math>  partial pr. at eq.</p><p>Thus, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mi>&#945;</mi><mn>2</mn></mfrac></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#8230;</mo><mfenced><mn>1</mn></mfenced></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">p</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mfenced><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></msub></mfenced><msup><mfenced><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></msub></mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>/</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow></msub></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>&#945;</mi></mrow></mfenced><msup><mfenced><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>&#945;</mi></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac></mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>/</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#945;</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup></math></p><p>since <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#945;</mi></math> is negligible w.r.t 1 thus, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mi>&#945;</mi><mo>&#8776;</mo><mn>1</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>&#945;</mi><msqrt><mi>&#945;</mi></msqrt></mrow><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><msup><mi>&#945;</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>/</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><msup><mn>2</mn><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>/</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>&#945;</mi><mo>=</mo><msup><mn>2</mn><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>/</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>/</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>/</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>&#945;</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>%</mo><mi>&#945;</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>%</mo></math></p>

### Q39. JEE Main 2022 (28 Jul Shift 2)

<p>At <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>600</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mo>,</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>mol</mi></math> of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>NO</mi></math> are mixed with <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>mol</mi></math> of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math>.</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><msub><mi>NO</mi><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mfenced></msub><mo>+</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mfenced><mo>&#8644;</mo><mn>2</mn><msub><mi>NO</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mfenced></math></p><p>The reaction occurring as above comes to equilibrium under a total pressure of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn></math> atm. Analysis of the system shows that <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>6</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>mol</mi></math> of oxygen are present at equilibrium. The equilibrium constant for the reaction is____.</p>

**Correct Value:** **2**

<blockquote><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mi>NO</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>+</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mtable><mtr><mtd><mn>2</mn></mtd><mtd><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>1</mn></mtd><mtd><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>-</mo></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>2</mn></mtd><mtd><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>6</mn></mtd><mtd><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>8</mn></mtd></mtr></mtable></math></p><p>Total number of moles=<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>6</mn></math></p><p>According to Dalton&#39;s law,</p><p>Partial pressure<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo></math><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"></math>Total pressure<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#215;</mo></math>Mole fraction</p><p>Mole fraction<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>Number</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>of</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>moles</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>of</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>one</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>component</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>Total</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>number</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>of</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>moles</mi></mrow></mfrac></math></p><p><math ### Q40. JEE Main 2022 (26 Jul Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

<p>At <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>298</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></math>, the equilibrium constant is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>15</mn></msup></math> for the reaction:</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Cu</mi><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><msup><mi>Ag</mi><mo>+</mo></msup><mfenced><mi>aq</mi></mfenced><mo>&#8652;</mo><msup><mi>Cu</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mfenced><mi>aq</mi></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>Ag</mi><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></mfenced></math></p><p>The equilibrium constant for the reaction</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><msup><mi>Cu</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mfenced><mi>aq</mi></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mi>Ag</mi><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></mfenced><mo>&#8652;</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>Cu</mi><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></mfenced><mo>+</mo><msup><mi>Ag</mi><mo>+</mo></msup><mfenced><mi>aq</mi></mfenced></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>8</mn></mrow></msup></math>. The value of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi></math> is ______. (Round off the answer to the nearest integer)</p>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

mathvariant="normal">s</mi></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><msup><mi>Ag</mi><mo>+</mo></msup><mfenced><mi>aq</mi></mfenced><mo>&#8652;</mo><msup><mi>Cu</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mfenced><mi>aq</mi></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>Ag</mi><mfenced><mi ### Q46. JEE Main 2021 (27 Aug Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

When <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>5</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math> of solid <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>4</mn></msub><mi>HS</mi></math> is introduced into a two litre evacuated flask at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>27</mn><mo>&#176;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>20</mn><mo>%</mo></math> of the solid decomposes into gaseous ammonia and hydrogen sulphide. The <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">p</mi></msub></math> for the reaction at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>27</mn><mo>&#176;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>. The value of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi></math> is (Integer answer)
[Given <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced close="]" open=""><mrow><mn>1</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>082</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>atm</mi><msup><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><msup><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>mol</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced></math>

### Q47. JEE Main 2021 (27 Aug Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

mathvariant="normal">x</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Ag</mi><mo>+</mo></msup><mo>(</mo><mi>aq</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mo>(</mo><mi>aq</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>&#8652;</mo><msup><mfenced ### Q49. JEE Main 2021 (26 Aug Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

In the sulphur estimation, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>471</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math> of an organic compound gave <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>44</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math> of barium sulfate. The percentage of sulphur in the compound is. <summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **42**

**Detailed Solution:**

</details>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>083</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>bar</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi>mol</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced></math>

<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Detailed Solution:**

[Assume all gases are ideal and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>0831</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi></math> bar <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced close="]" open=""><mrow><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><msup><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>mol</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced></math>
