### Q89. JEE Main 2013 (09 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

In reaction $\mathrm{A}+2 \mathrm{~B} \rightleftharpoons 2 \mathrm{C}+\mathrm{D}$, initial concentration of $\mathrm{B}$ was $1.5$ times of $\mathrm{[A]}$, but at equilibrium the concentrations of $\mathrm{A}$ and $\mathrm{B}$ became equal. The equilibrium constant for the reaction is :

**Options:**

- **(A)** $8$
- **(B)** $4$
- **(C)** $12$
- **(D)** $6$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $4$**

**Detailed Solution:**

<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/Fw8HbmRZMzOSnb9jQFNH5Sg6laNsCwfjy3UrY1N6VSU.original.fullsize.png">

Hence $\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{C}}=\frac{(2 x)^2 \times x}{(a-x)(1.5 a-2 x)^2}$
Given, at equilibrium
$\begin{array}{ll}\therefore & (a-x)=(1.5 a-2 x) \\ \therefore & a=2 x\end{array}$
On solving $K_C=4$

</details>

### Q90. JEE Main 2012 (26 May Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

One mole of $\mathrm{O}_{2(\mathrm{~g})}$ and two moles of $\mathrm{SO}_{2(\mathrm{~g})}$ were heated in a closed vessel of one-litre capacity at $1098 \mathrm{~K}$. At equilibrium $1.6$ moles of $\mathrm{SO}_{3(\mathrm{~g})}$ were found. The equilibrium constant $K_c$ of the reaction would be

**Options:**

- **(A)** 30
- **(B)** 40
- **(C)** 80
- **(D)** 60

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) 80**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\quad \mathrm{O}_2+2 \mathrm{SO}_2 \rightleftharpoons 2 \mathrm{SO}_3$ at $\mathrm{t}=0 \quad 1 \quad 2 \quad 0$ at equil. $(1-\alpha) 2(1-\alpha) \quad 2 \alpha$ Given at equilibrium
$$
\begin{aligned}
& 2 \alpha=1.6 \\
& K_{\mathrm{C}}=\frac{(2 \alpha)^2}{(1-\alpha)(2-2 \alpha)^2}=\frac{\begin{array}{c}
\alpha=0.8 \\
(0.8)^2
\end{array}}{(1-0.8)(1-0.8)^2} \\
& =\frac{0.64}{0.002} \\
& K_{\mathrm{C}}=80 \\
&
\end{aligned}
$$

</details>

### Q91. JEE Main 2012 (19 May Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

The value of $K_p$ for the equilibrium reaction $\mathrm{N}_2 \mathrm{O}_4(g) \rightleftharpoons 2 \mathrm{NO}_2(g)$ is 2 .
The percentage dissociation of $\mathrm{N}_2 \mathrm{O}_4(g)$ at a pressure of $0.5 \mathrm{~atm}$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** 25
- **(B)** 88
- **(C)** 50
- **(D)** 71

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) 71**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{array}{llcc}\text { } & \mathrm{N}_2 \mathrm{O}_4(\mathrm{~g}) \rightleftharpoons 2 \mathrm{NO}_2(\mathrm{~g}) \\ & \text { Initial moles } & 1 & 0 \\ & \text { Moles of equil. } & (1-\alpha) & 2 \alpha\end{array}$
( $\alpha=$ degree of dissociation $)$
Total number of moles at equil.
$$
\begin{aligned}
& =(1-\alpha)+2 \alpha \\
& =(1+\alpha) \\
p_{N_2 \mathrm{O}_4} & =\frac{(1-\alpha)}{(1+\alpha)} \times P \\
p_{N O_2} & =\frac{2 \alpha}{(1+\alpha)} \times P \\
K_P & =\frac{\left(p_{N_O}\right)^2}{p_{N_2 O_4}}=\frac{\left(\frac{2 \alpha}{(1+\alpha)} \times P\right)^2}{\left(\frac{1-\alpha}{1+\alpha}\right) \times P}=\frac{4 \alpha^2 P}{1-\alpha^2}
\end{aligned}
$$

$$
\begin{aligned}
& \text { Given, } K_P=2, P=0.5 \mathrm{~atm} \\
& \therefore \quad K_P=\frac{4 \alpha^2 P}{1-\alpha^2} \\
& =\frac{4 \alpha^2 \times 0.5}{1-\alpha^2} \\
& \alpha=0.707 \approx 0.71 \\
& \therefore \quad \text { Percentage dissociation } \\
& =0.71 \times 100=71
\end{aligned}
$$

</details>

### Q92. JEE Main 2012 (12 May Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

Given
(i) $\operatorname{HCN}(a q)+\mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{O}(b) \rightleftharpoons \mathrm{H}_3 \mathrm{O}^{+}(a q)+\mathrm{CN}^{-}(a q)$ $K_{\mathrm{a}}=6.2 \times 10^{-10}$
(ii) $\mathrm{CN}^{-}(a q)+\mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{O}(\mathrm{l}) \rightleftharpoons \mathrm{HCN}(a q)+\mathrm{OH}^{-}(a q)$ $K_{\mathrm{b}}=1.6 \times 10^{-5}$.
These equilibria show the following order of the relative base strength,

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\mathrm{OH}^{-}>\mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{O}>\mathrm{CN}^{-}$
- **(B)** $\mathrm{OH}^{-}>\mathrm{CN}^{-}>\mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{O}$
- **(C)** $\mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{O}>\mathrm{CN}^{-}>\mathrm{OH}$
- **(D)** $\mathrm{CN}^{-}>\mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{O}>\mathrm{OH}^{-}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $\mathrm{OH}^{-}>\mathrm{CN}^{-}>\mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{O}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The more is the value of equilibrium constant, the more is the completion of reaction or more is the concentration of products i.e. the order of relative strength would be
$$
\mathrm{OH}^{-}>\mathrm{CN}^{-}>\mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{O}
$$

</details>

### Q93. JEE Main 2012 (12 May Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

$8 \mathrm{~mol}$ of $A B_3(\mathrm{~g})$ are introduced into a $1.0 \mathrm{dm}^3$ vessel. If it dissociates as
$2 \mathrm{AB}_3(g) \rightleftharpoons A_2(g)+3 B_2(g)$. At equilibrium, $2 \mathrm{~mol}$ of $A_2$ are found to be present. The equilibrium constant of this reaction is

**Options:**

- **(A)** 2
- **(B)** 3
- **(C)** 27
- **(D)** 36

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) 27**

**Detailed Solution:**

$2 A B_3(g) \leftrightharpoons A_2(g)+3 B_2(g)$
at $t=0 \quad 8 \quad 0 \quad 0$
at eq. $(8-2 \times 2) \quad 2 \quad 3 \times 2$ $=4 \quad 2 \quad 6$
now $K_C=\frac{\left[A_2\right]\left[B_2\right]^3}{\left[A B_3\right]^2}=\frac{2 / 1 \times[6 / 1]^3}{[4 / 1]^2}=27$

</details>

### Q94. JEE Main 2012 (Offline)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

The equilibrium constant $\left(\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{c}}\right)$ for the reaction $\mathrm{N}_2(\mathrm{g})+\mathrm{O}_2(\mathrm{~g}) \rightarrow 2 \mathrm{NO}(\mathrm{g})$ at temperature $\mathrm{T}$ is $4 \times 10^{-4}$. The value of $\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{c}}$ for the reaction, $\mathrm{NO}(\mathrm{g}) \rightarrow{1 / 2} \mathrm{~N}_2(\mathrm{g})+{1 / 2} \mathrm{~O}_2(\mathrm{g})$ at the same temperature is :

**Options:**

- **(A)** $0.02$
- **(B)** $2.5 \times 10^2$
- **(C)** $4 \times 10^{-4}$
- **(D)** $50.0$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $50.0$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{array}{ll}\mathrm{N}_2+\mathrm{O}_2 \rightleftharpoons 2 \mathrm{NO} & \mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{C}}=4 \times 10^{-4} \\ \mathrm{NO} \rightleftharpoons \frac{1}{2} \mathrm{~N}_2+\frac{1}{2} \mathrm{O}_2 & \mathrm{~K}_{\mathrm{C}}^1=\sqrt{\frac{1}{\mathrm{~K}_{\mathrm{C}}}} \\ \mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{C}}^1=\frac{1}{\sqrt{4 \times 10^{-4}}}=50 & \end{array}$

</details>

### Q95. JEE Main 2011

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

A vessel at $1000 \mathrm{~K}$ contains $\mathrm{CO}_2$ with a pressure of $0.5 \mathrm{~atm}$. Some of the $\mathrm{CO}_2$ is converted into $\mathrm{CO}$ on the addition of graphite. If the total pressure at equilibrium is $0.8 \mathrm{~atm}$, the value of $\mathrm{K}$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $3 \mathrm{~atm}$
- **(B)** $0.3 \mathrm{~atm}$
- **(C)** $0.18 \mathrm{~atm}$
- **(D)** $1.8 \mathrm{~atm}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $1.8 \mathrm{~atm}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/TfD6wcBuYicNjQx3AahyQMG7Z9peOwV6f1b0Hpagts4.original.fullsize.png">

Total pressure at equilibrium $=0.8 \mathrm{~atm}$; Total no.of moles $=p+x$.
Therefore $\mathrm{p} \propto \mathrm{n} ; \frac{0.5}{0.8}=\frac{\mathrm{p}}{\mathrm{p}+\mathrm{x}} \Rightarrow \mathrm{x}=0.3$
$$
\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{p}}=\frac{\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{CO}}^2}{\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{CO}_2}}=\frac{0.6 \times 0.6}{0.2}=1.8 \mathrm{~atm}
$$

</details>

### Q96. JEE Main 2010

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

In aqueous solution the ionization constants for carbonic acid are $\mathrm{K}_1=4.2 \times 10^{-7}$ and $\mathrm{K}_2=4.8 \times 10^{-11}$
Select the correct statement for a saturated $0.034 \mathrm{M}$ solution of the carbonic acid.

**Options:**

- **(A)** The concentration of $\mathrm{CO}_3^{2-}$ is $0.034 \mathrm{M}$.
- **(B)** The concentration of $\mathrm{CO}_3^{2-}$ is greater than that of $\mathrm{HCO}_3^{-}$.
- **(C)** The concentration of $\mathrm{H}^{+}$and $\mathrm{HCO}_3^{-}$are approximately equal.
- **(D)** The concentration of $\mathrm{H}^{+}$is double that of $\mathrm{CO}_3^{2-}$.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) The concentration of $\mathrm{H}^{+}$and $\mathrm{HCO}_3^{-}$are approximately equal.**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{array}{lll}\mathrm{A} \rightarrow & \mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{CO}_3 \rightleftharpoons \mathrm{H}^{+}+\mathrm{HCO}_3^{-} & \mathrm{K}_1=4.2 \times 10^{-7} \\ \mathrm{~B} \rightarrow & \mathrm{HCO}_3^{-} \rightleftharpoons \mathrm{H}^{+}+\mathrm{CO}_3^{-2} & \mathrm{~K}_2=4.8 \times 10^{-11}\end{array}$
As $\mathrm{K}_2 \ll \mathrm{K}_1$
All major $\left[\mathrm{H}^{+}\right]_{\text {todal }} \approx\left[\mathrm{H}^{+}\right]_{\mathrm{A}}$
and from I equilibrium, $\left[\mathrm{H}^{+}\right]_{\mathrm{A}} \approx\left[\mathrm{HCO}_3^{-}\right] \approx\left[\mathrm{H}^{+}\right]_{\text {tocal }}$
$\left[\mathrm{CO}_3^{-2}\right]$ is negligible compared to $\left[\mathrm{HCO}_3^{-}\right]$or $\left[\mathrm{H}^{+}\right]_{\text {total }}$

</details>

### Q97. JEE Main 2008

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

For the following three reactions $a, b$ and $c$, equilibrium constants are given:
a. $\mathrm{CO}(\mathrm{g})+\mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{O}(\mathrm{g}) \rightleftharpoons \mathrm{CO}_2(\mathrm{~g})+\mathrm{H}_2(\mathrm{~g}) ; \quad \mathrm{K}_1$
b. $\mathrm{CH}_4(\mathrm{~g})+\mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{O}(\mathrm{g}) \rightleftharpoons \mathrm{CO}(\mathrm{g})+3 \mathrm{H}_2(\mathrm{~g}) ; \quad \mathrm{K}_2$
c. $\mathrm{CH}_4(\mathrm{~g})+2 \mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{O}(\mathrm{g}) \rightleftharpoons \mathrm{CO}_2(\mathrm{~g})+4 \mathrm{H}_2(\mathrm{~g}) ; \mathrm{K}_3$
Which of the following relations is correct?

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\mathrm{K}_1 \sqrt{\mathrm{K}_2}=\mathrm{K}_3$
- **(B)** $\mathrm{K}_2 \mathrm{~K}_3=\mathrm{K}_1$
- **(C)** $\mathrm{K}_3=\mathrm{K}_1 \mathrm{~K}_2$
- **(D)** $\mathrm{K}_3 \cdot \mathrm{K}_2^3=\mathrm{K}_1^2$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $\mathrm{K}_3=\mathrm{K}_1 \mathrm{~K}_2$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Equation (c) = equation (a) + equation (b)
Thus $\mathrm{K}_3=\mathrm{K}_1 \cdot \mathrm{K}_2$

</details>

### Q98. JEE Main 2008

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

The equilibrium constants $K_{P_1}$ and $K_{P_2}$ for the reactions $X \rightleftharpoons 2 Y$ and $Z \rightleftharpoons P+Q$, respectively are in the ratio of $1: 9$. If the degree of dissociation of $X$ and $Z$ be equal then the ratio of total pressure at these equilibria is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $1 : 36$
- **(B)** $1 : 1$
- **(C)** $1 : 3$
- **(D)** $1 : 9$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $1 : 36$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$$
\begin{aligned}
& X \rightleftharpoons 2 Y \\
& 10 \\
& (1-x) \quad 2 x \\
& k_{p_1}=\frac{(2 x)^2}{(1-x)}\left(\frac{P_1}{1+x}\right)^1 \\
& Z \rightleftharpoons P+Q \\
& 100 \\
& (1-x) \quad x \quad x \\
& \mathrm{k}_{\mathrm{p}_2}=\frac{\mathrm{x}^2}{(1-\mathrm{x})}\left(\frac{\mathrm{P}_2}{1+\mathrm{x}}\right)^1 \\
& \frac{4 \times \mathrm{P}_1}{\mathrm{P}_2}=\frac{1}{9} \Rightarrow \frac{\mathrm{P}_1}{\mathrm{P}_2}=\frac{1}{36} \\
&
\end{aligned}
$$

</details>

### Q99. JEE Main 2006

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

The equilibrium constant for the reaction
$$
\mathrm{SO}_3(\mathrm{~g}) \rightleftharpoons \mathrm{SO}_2(\mathrm{~g})+\frac{1}{2} \mathrm{O}_2(\mathrm{~g})
$$
is $\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{c}}=4.9 \times 10^{-2}$. The value of $\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{c}}$ for the reaction
$$
2 \mathrm{SO}_2(\mathrm{~g})+\mathrm{O}_2(\mathrm{~g}) \rightleftharpoons 2 \mathrm{SO}_3(\mathrm{~g})
$$ 
will be

**Options:**

- **(A)** 416
- **(B)** $2.40 \times 10^{-3}$
- **(C)** $9.8 \times 10^{-2}$
- **(D)** $4.9 \times 10^{-2}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) 416**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{aligned} \mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{c}}^{\prime} & =\left(\frac{1}{4.9 \times 10^{-2}}\right)^2 \\ & =\frac{10^4}{4.9 \times 4.9}=\frac{100 \times 100}{24.01} \\ & =4.1649 \times 100 \\ & =416.49\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q100. JEE Main 2005

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

An amount of solid $\mathrm{NH}_4 \mathrm{HS}$ is placed in a flask already containing ammonia gas at a certain temperature and $0.50 \mathrm{~atm}$. Pressure. Ammonium hydrogen sulphide decomposes to yield $\mathrm{NH}_3$ and $\mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{~S}$ gases in the flask. When the decomposition reaction reaches equilibrium, the total pressure in the flask rises to $0.84 \mathrm{~atm}$. The equilibrium constant for $\mathrm{NH}_4 \mathrm{HS}$ decomposition at this temperature is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $0.30$
- **(B)** $0.18$
- **(C)** $0.17$
- **(D)** $0.11$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $0.11$**

**Detailed Solution:**

<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/Kvaw0RT8zSGaN36SL60ZA_lD0fudaidmAR4c7D8cXcU.original.fullsize.png">

Total pressure $=0.5+2 \mathrm{x}=0.84$
i.e., $\mathrm{x}=0.17$
$\mathrm{~K}_{\mathrm{p}}=\mathrm{p}_{\mathrm{NH}_3} \cdot \mathrm{p}_{\mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{~s}}$
$=(0.67) .(0.17)$
$\quad=0.1139$.

### Q101. JEE Main 2005

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

For the reaction
$$
\begin{aligned}
& 2 \mathrm{NO}_{2(\mathrm{~g})} \rightleftharpoons 2 \mathrm{NO}_{(\mathrm{g})}+\mathrm{O}_{2(\mathrm{~g})} \text {, } \\
& \left(\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{c}}=1.8 \times 10^{-6} \text {at } 184 \mathrm{C}\right) \\
& (\mathrm{R}=0.0831 \mathrm{~kJ} /(\mathrm{mol} . \mathrm{K})
\end{aligned}
$$
When $\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{p}}$ and $\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{c}}$ are compared at $184^{\circ} \mathrm{C}$, it is found that

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{p}}$ is greater than $\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{c}}$
- **(B)** $\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{p}}$ is less than $\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{c}}$
- **(C)** $\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{p}}=\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{c}}$
- **(D)** Whether $\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{p}}$ is greater than, less than or equal to $\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{c}}$ depends upon the total gas pressure

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{p}}$ is greater than $\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{c}}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$$
\mathrm{Kp}=\mathrm{Kc} \mathrm{RT} ^{\Delta n}, \quad \Delta \mathrm{n}=1
$$
$$
\mathrm{Kp}>\mathrm{Kc}
$$

</details>

### Q102. JEE Main 2004

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

The equilibrium constant for the reaction $\mathrm{N}_2(\mathrm{~g})+\mathrm{O}_2(\mathrm{~g}) \rightleftharpoons 2 \mathrm{NO}(\mathrm{g})$ at temperature $\mathrm{T}$ is $4 \times 10^{-4}$. The value of $\mathrm{Kc}$ for the reaction $\mathrm{NO}(\mathrm{g}) \rightleftharpoons \frac{1}{2} \mathrm{~N}_2(\mathrm{~g})+\frac{1}{2} \mathrm{O}_2(\mathrm{~g})$ at the same temperature is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $2.5 \times 10^2$
- **(B)** $0.02$
- **(C)** $4 \times 10^{-4}$
- **(D)** 50

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) 50**

**Detailed Solution:**

As per property of equilibria reverse the equation \& divide it by 2

</details>

### Q103. JEE Main 2004

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

For the reaction, $\mathrm{CO}(\mathrm{g})+\mathrm{Cl}_2(\mathrm{~g}) \rightleftharpoons \mathrm{COCl}_2(\mathrm{~g})$ the $\frac{\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{p}}}{\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{c}}}$ is equal to

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\frac{1}{\mathrm{RT}}$
- **(B)** $1.0$
- **(C)** $\sqrt{R T}$
- **(D)** $\mathrm{RT}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $\frac{1}{\mathrm{RT}}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\mathrm{Kp}=\mathrm{Kc}(\mathrm{RT})^{\Delta \mathrm{n}}$

</details>

### Q104. JEE Main 2004

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

What is the equilibrium expression for the reaction $\mathrm{P}_{4(\mathrm{~s})}+5 \mathrm{O}_{2(\mathrm{~g})} \rightleftharpoons \mathrm{P}_4 \mathrm{O}_{10(\mathrm{~s}}$ ?

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\left.\mathrm{Kc}=\left[\mathrm{P}_4 \mathrm{O}_{10}\right] / \mathrm{P}_4\right]\left[\mathrm{O}_2\right]^5$
- **(B)** $\mathrm{Kc}=1 /\left[\mathrm{O}_2\right]^5$
- **(C)** $\mathrm{Kc}=\left[\mathrm{O}_2\right]^5$
- **(D)** $\mathrm{Kc}=\left[\mathrm{P}_4 \mathrm{O}_{10}\right] / 5\left[\mathrm{P}_4\right]\left[\mathrm{O}_2\right]$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $\mathrm{Kc}=1 /\left[\mathrm{O}_2\right]^5$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Applying law of mass action

</details>

### Q105. JEE Main 2003

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

For the reaction equilibrium $\mathrm{N}_2 \mathrm{O}_4(\mathrm{~g}) \rightleftharpoons 2 \mathrm{NO}_2(\mathrm{~g})$ the concentrations of $\mathrm{N}_2 \mathrm{O}_4$ and $\mathrm{NO}_2$ at equilibrium are $4.8 \times 10^{-2}$ and $1.2 \times 10^{-2} \mathrm{~mol} \mathrm{~L}^{-1}$ respectively. The value of $\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{c}}$ for the reaction is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $3 \times 10^{-1} \mathrm{~mol} \mathrm{~L}^{-1}$
- **(B)** $3 \times 10^{-3} \mathrm{~mol} \mathrm{~L}^{-1}$
- **(C)** $3 \times 10^3 \mathrm{~mol} \mathrm{~L}^{-1}$
- **(D)** $3.3 \times 10^2 \mathrm{~mol} \mathrm{~L}^{-1}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $3 \times 10^{-3} \mathrm{~mol} \mathrm{~L}^{-1}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{C}}=\frac{\left[\mathrm{NO}_2\right]^2}{\left[\mathrm{~N}_2 \mathrm{O}_4\right]}=\frac{\left[1.2 \times 10^{-2}\right]^2}{\left[4.8 \times 10^{-2}\right]}=3 \times 10^{-3} \mathrm{~mol} / \mathrm{L}$

</details>

---

### Q106. JEE Main 2002

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

For the reaction $\mathrm{CO}(\mathrm{g})+(1 / 2) \mathrm{O}_2(\mathrm{~g})=\mathrm{CO}_2(\mathrm{~g}), \mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{p}} / \mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{c}}$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\mathrm{RT}$
- **(B)** $(\mathrm{RT})^{-1}$
- **(C)** $(R T)^{-1 / 2}$
- **(D)** $(R T)^{1 / 2}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $(R T)^{-1 / 2}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{p}}=\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{c}}(\mathrm{RT})^{\Delta n} ; \Delta \mathrm{n}=1-\left(1+\frac{1}{2}\right)=1-\frac{3}{2}=-\frac{1}{2}$
$\therefore \frac{\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{p}}}{\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{c}}}=(\mathrm{RT})^{-1 / 2}$

</details>

---
